.
∎

E.2
Proof of Theorem
4

In this section, we analyze the asymptotic behavior of the generalization error of KRR when
f^{\prime}(0)=f^{(3)}(0)=0
in the approximated kernel (
10
) and
f_{*}({\boldsymbol{x}})={\boldsymbol{x}}^{\top}{\boldsymbol{G}}{\boldsymbol{x}}/d
is a pure quadratic function where
{\boldsymbol{G}}\in\mathbb{R}^{d\times d}
is a symmetric random matrix satisfying

\mathbb{E}[{\boldsymbol{G}}_{i,j}]=0,\penalty\ \mathbb{E}[{\boldsymbol{G}}_{i,j}^{2}]=1
for all
i,j\in[n]
.
Hence, under the settings of Theorem
4
, the prediction risk of KRR defined in (
27
) can written as

\displaystyle\mathcal{R}(\lambda)=\penalty\

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{{\boldsymbol{x}},{\boldsymbol{G}}}[|{\boldsymbol{f}}_{*}({\boldsymbol{x}})|^{2}]+\operatorname{Tr}(\boldsymbol{K}+\lambda{\mathbf{I}})^{-1}{\boldsymbol{M}}(\boldsymbol{K}+\lambda{\mathbf{I}})^{-1}\mathbb{E}_{{\boldsymbol{G}}}[{\boldsymbol{f}}_{*}{\boldsymbol{f}}_{*}^{\top}]

(242)

\displaystyle+\sigma^{2}_{\boldsymbol{\epsilon}}\operatorname{Tr}(\boldsymbol{K}+\lambda{\mathbf{I}})^{-1}{\boldsymbol{M}}(\boldsymbol{K}+\lambda{\mathbf{I}})^{-1}-2\operatorname{Tr}(\boldsymbol{K}+\lambda{\mathbf{I}})^{-1}{\boldsymbol{V}}.

(243)

where we only take expectation with respect to
{\boldsymbol{G}}
, test data point
{\boldsymbol{x}}
and noise
\boldsymbol{\epsilon}
. In (
243
),
{\boldsymbol{M}}
is defined in Lemma
35
,
{\boldsymbol{f}}_{*}:=[f_{*}({\boldsymbol{x}}_{1}),\ldots,f_{*}({\boldsymbol{x}}_{n})]^{\top}
, with
f_{*}({\boldsymbol{x}}_{i})=\frac{1}{d}{\boldsymbol{x}}_{i}^{\top}{\boldsymbol{G}}{\boldsymbol{x}}_{i}
and

{\boldsymbol{V}}:=\mathbb{E}[{\boldsymbol{f}}_{*}f_{*}({\boldsymbol{x}})K({\boldsymbol{X}},{\boldsymbol{x}})|{\boldsymbol{X}}]\in\mathbb{R}^{n\times n}
,
where
K({\boldsymbol{X}},{\boldsymbol{x}})=[K({\boldsymbol{x}}_{1},{\boldsymbol{x}}),\dots,K({\boldsymbol{x}}_{n},{\boldsymbol{x}})]\in\mathbb{R}^{n}
. Notice that for any
i,j\in[n]
,

{\boldsymbol{V}}_{i,j}=\mathbb{E}[K({\boldsymbol{x}},{\boldsymbol{x}}_{j})f_{*}({\boldsymbol{x}})f_{*}({\boldsymbol{x}}_{i})|{\boldsymbol{X}}]
.
Furthermore, Assumption
2.3.2
provides a simpler approximation of
{\boldsymbol{M}}
, and

\widetilde{\boldsymbol{b}}_{0}=b_{0}\mathbf{1}+\frac{f^{(2)}(0)}{2d^{2}}\boldsymbol{\mu},\quad\widetilde{\boldsymbol{b}}_{1}=0,\quad a_{1}=0,

(244)

where
\boldsymbol{\mu}
is defined in (
230
), and
\widetilde{\boldsymbol{b}}_{0}
and
\widetilde{\boldsymbol{b}}_{1}
are defined by (
216
).

Lemma 40
.

Under the same assumptions as Theorem
1
, we have that

\|{\boldsymbol{V}}-{\boldsymbol{V}}^{(2)}\|\leq\penalty\ \frac{c}{d^{2.4}},

with probability at least
1-O(d^{-1/48})
for some constant
c>0
, where

{\boldsymbol{V}}^{(2)}:=\frac{1}{d^{2}}(\boldsymbol{\mu}{\boldsymbol{b}}_{0}^{\top}+2a_{2}{\boldsymbol{M}}_{0}^{(2)})

(245)

and
{\boldsymbol{b}}_{0}
,
{\boldsymbol{M}}_{0}^{(2)}
, and
\boldsymbol{\mu}
are defined by (
215
), (
217
), and (
230
).

Proof.

For any
j,i\in[n]
, by the definition of
f_{*}({\boldsymbol{x}})
, we have

\displaystyle{\boldsymbol{V}}_{j,i}

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}_{[}K({\boldsymbol{x}},{\boldsymbol{x}}_{i})f_{*}({\boldsymbol{x}})f_{*}({\boldsymbol{x}}_{j})|{\boldsymbol{X}}]

(246)

\displaystyle=\sum_{k=0}^{8}b_{k,i}\mathbb{E}_{{\boldsymbol{G}}}[\mathbb{E}_{{\boldsymbol{x}}}[{\boldsymbol{T}}_{i}^{(k)}f_{*}({\boldsymbol{x}})]f_{*}({\boldsymbol{x}}_{j})]+\mathbb{E}_{{\boldsymbol{x}},{\boldsymbol{G}}}\left[\frac{f^{(9)}(\zeta_{i})}{9!d^{9}}f_{*}({\boldsymbol{x}}_{j})f_{*}({\boldsymbol{x}})\langle{\boldsymbol{x}}_{i},{\boldsymbol{x}}\rangle^{9}\right]

(247)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{d^{2}}{\boldsymbol{x}}_{j}^{\top}{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}{\boldsymbol{x}}_{j}b_{0,i}+\frac{b_{2,i}}{d}\mathbb{E}_{{\boldsymbol{G}}}[f_{*}({\boldsymbol{x}}_{j}){\boldsymbol{x}}_{i}^{\top}{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}{\boldsymbol{G}}{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}{\boldsymbol{x}}_{i}]+\mathbb{E}_{{\boldsymbol{x}},{\boldsymbol{G}}}\left[\frac{f^{(9)}(\zeta_{i})}{9!d^{9}}f_{*}({\boldsymbol{x}}_{j})f_{*}({\boldsymbol{x}})\langle{\boldsymbol{x}}_{i},{\boldsymbol{x}}\rangle^{9}\right]

(248)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{d^{2}}{\boldsymbol{x}}_{j}^{\top}{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}{\boldsymbol{x}}_{j}b_{0,i}+\frac{2b_{2,i}}{d^{2}}({\boldsymbol{x}}_{j}^{\top}{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}{\boldsymbol{x}}_{i})^{2}+\mathbb{E}_{{\boldsymbol{x}},{\boldsymbol{G}}}\left[\frac{f^{(9)}(\zeta_{i})}{9!d^{9}}f_{*}({\boldsymbol{x}}_{j})f_{*}({\boldsymbol{x}})\langle{\boldsymbol{x}}_{i},{\boldsymbol{x}}\rangle^{9}\right]

(249)

where in the second line we applied (
204
), Lemmas
33
and
20
.
Therefore,

\displaystyle\|{\boldsymbol{V}}-{\boldsymbol{V}}^{(2)}\|\leq\penalty\

\displaystyle\frac{2}{d^{2}}\|({\boldsymbol{X}}{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}{\boldsymbol{X}}^{\top})^{\odot 2}\|\cdot\max_{i\in[n]}|a_{2}-b_{2,i}|+\frac{n}{d^{11}}\max_{i,j\in[n]}|\mathbb{E}_{{\boldsymbol{x}},{\boldsymbol{G}}}[{\boldsymbol{x}}^{\top}{\boldsymbol{G}}{\boldsymbol{x}}{\boldsymbol{x}}_{j}^{\top}{\boldsymbol{G}}{\boldsymbol{x}}_{j}({\boldsymbol{x}}_{i}^{\top}{\boldsymbol{x}})^{9}]|

(250)

\displaystyle\lesssim\penalty\

\displaystyle\frac{1}{d^{5.4}}\|({\boldsymbol{X}}{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}{\boldsymbol{X}}^{\top})^{\odot 2}\|+\frac{1}{d^{9}}\max_{i,j\in[n]}|\mathbb{E}_{{\boldsymbol{x}}}[({\boldsymbol{x}}^{\top}{\boldsymbol{x}}_{j})^{2}({\boldsymbol{x}}_{i}^{\top}{\boldsymbol{x}})^{9}]|

(251)

\displaystyle\lesssim\penalty\

\displaystyle\frac{1}{d^{2.4}}+\frac{1}{d^{9}}\max_{i,j\in[n]}\|{\boldsymbol{w}}_{j}\|^{2}\cdot\|{\boldsymbol{w}}_{j}\|^{9}\lesssim d^{-2.4},

(252)

with probability at least
1-O(d^{-\frac{1}{48}})
, where we utilize Lemmas
32
and
34
, and the definition of
f_{*}
. This completes the proof of the lemma.
∎

In the following lemma, we further approximate each term in
\bar{}\mathcal{R}(\lambda)
. Define

\displaystyle\widetilde{}\mathcal{R}(\lambda):=\penalty\

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[|f_{*}({\boldsymbol{x}})|^{2}]+\operatorname{Tr}(\boldsymbol{K}+\lambda{\mathbf{I}})^{-1}{\boldsymbol{M}}^{(2)}(\boldsymbol{K}+\lambda{\mathbf{I}})^{-1}\mathbb{E}_{{\boldsymbol{G}}}[{\boldsymbol{f}}_{*}{\boldsymbol{f}}_{*}^{\top}]

\displaystyle+\sigma^{2}_{\boldsymbol{\epsilon}}\operatorname{Tr}(\boldsymbol{K}+\lambda{\mathbf{I}})^{-1}{\boldsymbol{M}}^{(2)}(\boldsymbol{K}+\lambda{\mathbf{I}})^{-1}-2\operatorname{Tr}(\boldsymbol{K}+\lambda{\mathbf{I}})^{-1}{\boldsymbol{V}}^{(2)}.

(253)

Lemma 41
.

Under the same assumptions as Theorem
3
, for any
\lambda\geq 0
, we have that

|\mathcal{R}(\lambda)-\widetilde{}\mathcal{R}(\lambda)|\leq cd^{{-}\frac{1}{4}},

conditioning on
{\boldsymbol{G}}
in
f_{*}
defined in (
25
), with probability at least
1-O(d^{-1/48})
, for some
c>0
, where
\mathcal{R}(\lambda)
is defined by (
243
).

Proof.

Notice that

\mathbb{E}_{{\boldsymbol{G}}}[\|{\boldsymbol{f}}_{*}\|^{2}]=\frac{1}{d^{2}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbb{E}_{{\boldsymbol{G}}}[({\boldsymbol{x}}_{i}^{\top}{\boldsymbol{G}}{\boldsymbol{x}}_{i})^{2}]\lesssim\max_{i\in[n]}\|{\boldsymbol{x}}_{i}\|^{4}\lesssim d^{2}
, with probability at least
1-O(d^{-1})
,
because of (
85
).
Applying Lemmas
35
and
40
, we can get

\displaystyle\Big|\widetilde{}\mathcal{R}(\lambda)-\mathcal{R}(\lambda)\Big|\leq\penalty\

\displaystyle\Big|\operatorname{Tr}\boldsymbol{K}_{\lambda}^{-1}({\boldsymbol{M}}^{(2)}-{\boldsymbol{M}})\boldsymbol{K}_{\lambda}^{-1}\mathbb{E}_{{\boldsymbol{G}}}[{\boldsymbol{f}}_{*}{\boldsymbol{f}}_{*}^{\top}]\Big|+2\Big|\operatorname{Tr}\boldsymbol{K}_{\lambda}^{-1}({\boldsymbol{V}}^{(2)}-{\boldsymbol{V}})\Big|

(254)

\displaystyle+\sigma^{2}_{\boldsymbol{\epsilon}}\Big|\operatorname{Tr}\boldsymbol{K}_{\lambda}^{-1}({\boldsymbol{M}}^{(2)}-{\boldsymbol{M}})\boldsymbol{K}_{\lambda}^{-1}\Big|

(255)

\displaystyle\leq\penalty\

\displaystyle(n\sigma^{2}_{\boldsymbol{\epsilon}}+\mathbb{E}_{{\boldsymbol{G}}}[\|{\boldsymbol{f}}_{*}\|^{2}])\|\boldsymbol{K}_{\lambda}^{-1}\|^{2}\|{\boldsymbol{M}}^{(2)}-{\boldsymbol{M}}\|+2n\|\boldsymbol{K}_{\lambda}^{-1}\|\|{\boldsymbol{V}}^{(2)}-{\boldsymbol{V}}\|\lesssim d^{\frac{-1}{4}},

(256)

with probability
1-O(d^{-1/48})
, where in the last line, we utilize (
109
) and Lemma
25
.
∎

Hence, below, we will analyze
\widetilde{}\mathcal{R}(\lambda)
instead of prediction risk
\mathcal{R}(\lambda)
.

Lemma 42
.

Under the assumptions of Theorem
4
, we have
|\widetilde{}\mathcal{R}(\lambda)-(\sigma_{\varepsilon}^{2}{\mathcal{V}}+\mathcal{B})|\lesssim d^{-0.4}
with probability at least
1-O(d^{-1/48})
, where

\displaystyle{\mathcal{V}}:=\penalty\

\displaystyle 2a_{2}^{2}\operatorname{Tr}(\boldsymbol{K}+\lambda{\mathbf{I}})^{-1}{\boldsymbol{M}}_{0}^{(2)}(\boldsymbol{K}+\lambda{\mathbf{I}})^{-1}

(257)

\displaystyle\mathcal{B}:=\penalty\

\displaystyle\frac{2}{d^{2}}(\operatorname{Tr}{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}})^{2}+\frac{4a_{2}^{2}}{d^{2}}\operatorname{Tr}\boldsymbol{K}_{\lambda}^{-1}{\boldsymbol{M}}_{0}^{(2)}\boldsymbol{K}_{\lambda}^{-1}({\boldsymbol{X}}{\boldsymbol{X}}^{\top})^{\odot 2}-\frac{4a_{2}}{d^{2}}\operatorname{Tr}{\boldsymbol{X}}^{(2)}{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}^{(2)}{\boldsymbol{X}}^{(2)\top}\boldsymbol{K}_{\lambda}^{-1}.

(258)

Proof.

Recall the assumption of
{\boldsymbol{G}}
in
f_{*}({\boldsymbol{x}})={\boldsymbol{x}}^{\top}{\boldsymbol{G}}{\boldsymbol{x}}/d
from Theorem
4
. By taking expectation for
{\boldsymbol{G}}
, we can easily simplify the expression of
\widetilde{}\mathcal{R}(\lambda)
.
Notice that given any deterministic matrix
{\boldsymbol{A}}\in\mathbb{R}^{n\times n}
, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{{\boldsymbol{G}}}[{\boldsymbol{f}}_{*}^{\top}{\boldsymbol{A}}{\boldsymbol{f}}_{*}|{\boldsymbol{X}}]=\penalty\

\displaystyle\frac{2}{d^{2}}\operatorname{Tr}{\boldsymbol{A}}{\boldsymbol{X}}^{(2)}{\boldsymbol{X}}^{(2)\top}-\frac{1}{d^{2}}\sum_{k=1}^{d}\boldsymbol{\nu}_{k}^{\top}{\boldsymbol{A}}\boldsymbol{\nu}_{k},

(259)

where
\boldsymbol{\nu}_{k}\in\mathbb{R}^{n}
are defined by Lemma
36
. Considering (
89
), Lemma
36
and (
244
), we have

\displaystyle\widetilde{}\mathcal{R}(\lambda)=\penalty\

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[|f_{*}({\boldsymbol{x}})|^{2}]+\sigma^{2}_{\boldsymbol{\epsilon}}\operatorname{Tr}(\boldsymbol{K}+\lambda{\mathbf{I}})^{-1}{\boldsymbol{M}}^{(2)}(\boldsymbol{K}+\lambda{\mathbf{I}})^{-1}

(260)

\displaystyle+2a_{2}^{2}\operatorname{Tr}(\boldsymbol{K}+\lambda{\mathbf{I}})^{-1}{\boldsymbol{M}}^{(2)}(\boldsymbol{K}+\lambda{\mathbf{I}})^{-1}\mathbb{E}[{\boldsymbol{f}}_{*}{\boldsymbol{f}}_{*}^{\top}|{\boldsymbol{X}}]-2\operatorname{Tr}(\boldsymbol{K}+\lambda{\mathbf{I}})^{-1}{\boldsymbol{V}}^{(2)}

(261)

\displaystyle=\penalty\

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[|f_{*}({\boldsymbol{x}})|^{2}]+2a_{2}^{2}\sigma^{2}_{\boldsymbol{\epsilon}}\operatorname{Tr}\boldsymbol{K}_{\lambda}^{-1}{\boldsymbol{M}}_{0}^{(2)}\boldsymbol{K}_{\lambda}^{-1}

(262)

\displaystyle+2a_{2}^{2}\operatorname{Tr}\boldsymbol{K}_{\lambda}^{-1}{\boldsymbol{M}}_{0}^{(2)}\boldsymbol{K}_{\lambda}^{-1}\mathbb{E}[{\boldsymbol{f}}_{*}{\boldsymbol{f}}_{*}^{\top}|{\boldsymbol{X}}]-\frac{4a_{2}}{d^{2}}\operatorname{Tr}\boldsymbol{K}_{\lambda}^{-1}{\boldsymbol{M}}_{0}^{(2)}

(263)

\displaystyle+{\boldsymbol{b}}_{0}^{\top}\boldsymbol{K}_{\lambda}^{-1}\mathbb{E}[{\boldsymbol{f}}_{*}{\boldsymbol{f}}_{*}^{\top}|{\boldsymbol{X}}]\boldsymbol{K}_{\lambda}^{-1}{\boldsymbol{b}}_{0}-\frac{2}{d^{2}}{\boldsymbol{b}}_{0}^{\top}\boldsymbol{K}_{\lambda}^{-1}\boldsymbol{\mu}

(264)

\displaystyle+\operatorname{Tr}\boldsymbol{K}_{\lambda}^{-1}\text{diag}({\boldsymbol{b}}_{1}-\widetilde{\boldsymbol{b}}_{1}){\boldsymbol{X}}{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}{\boldsymbol{X}}^{\top}\text{diag}({\boldsymbol{b}}_{1}-\widetilde{\boldsymbol{b}}_{1})\boldsymbol{K}_{\lambda}^{-1}\mathbb{E}[{\boldsymbol{f}}_{*}{\boldsymbol{f}}_{*}^{\top}|{\boldsymbol{X}}]

(265)

\displaystyle+\sigma^{2}_{\boldsymbol{\epsilon}}\operatorname{Tr}\boldsymbol{K}_{\lambda}^{-1}{\boldsymbol{b}}_{0}{\boldsymbol{b}}_{0}^{\top}\boldsymbol{K}_{\lambda}^{-1}+\sigma^{2}_{\boldsymbol{\epsilon}}\operatorname{Tr}\boldsymbol{K}_{\lambda}^{-1}\text{diag}({\boldsymbol{b}}_{1}-\widetilde{\boldsymbol{b}}_{1}){\boldsymbol{X}}{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}{\boldsymbol{X}}^{\top}\text{diag}({\boldsymbol{b}}_{1}-\widetilde{\boldsymbol{b}}_{1})\boldsymbol{K}_{\lambda}^{-1}

(266)

\displaystyle=\penalty\

\displaystyle\sigma^{2}_{\boldsymbol{\epsilon}}{\mathcal{V}}+\mathcal{B}+\mathcal{R}_{\textnormal{mix}}-J_{1}+J_{2},

(267)

where

\displaystyle\mathcal{R}_{\textnormal{mix}}:=\penalty\

\displaystyle\frac{1}{d^{2}}\operatorname{Tr}({\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}^{2})+{\boldsymbol{b}}_{0}^{\top}\boldsymbol{K}_{\lambda}^{-1}\mathbb{E}[{\boldsymbol{f}}_{*}{\boldsymbol{f}}_{*}^{\top}|{\boldsymbol{X}}]\boldsymbol{K}_{\lambda}^{-1}{\boldsymbol{b}}_{0}-\frac{2}{d^{2}}{\boldsymbol{b}}_{0}^{\top}\boldsymbol{K}_{\lambda}^{-1}\boldsymbol{\mu}

(268)

\displaystyle+\operatorname{Tr}\boldsymbol{K}_{\lambda}^{-1}\text{diag}({\boldsymbol{b}}_{1}-\widetilde{\boldsymbol{b}}_{1}){\boldsymbol{X}}{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}{\boldsymbol{X}}^{\top}\text{diag}({\boldsymbol{b}}_{1}-\widetilde{\boldsymbol{b}}_{1})\boldsymbol{K}_{\lambda}^{-1}\mathbb{E}[{\boldsymbol{f}}_{*}{\boldsymbol{f}}_{*}^{\top}|{\boldsymbol{X}}]

(269)

\displaystyle+\sigma^{2}_{\boldsymbol{\epsilon}}\operatorname{Tr}\boldsymbol{K}_{\lambda}^{-1}{\boldsymbol{b}}_{0}{\boldsymbol{b}}_{0}^{\top}\boldsymbol{K}_{\lambda}^{-1}+\sigma^{2}_{\boldsymbol{\epsilon}}\operatorname{Tr}\boldsymbol{K}_{\lambda}^{-1}\text{diag}({\boldsymbol{b}}_{1}-\widetilde{\boldsymbol{b}}_{1}){\boldsymbol{X}}{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}{\boldsymbol{X}}^{\top}\text{diag}({\boldsymbol{b}}_{1}-\widetilde{\boldsymbol{b}}_{1})\boldsymbol{K}_{\lambda}^{-1}

(270)

\displaystyle J_{1}:=\penalty\

\displaystyle\frac{2a_{2}^{2}}{d^{2}}\sum_{k=1}^{d}\boldsymbol{\nu}_{k}^{\top}\boldsymbol{K}_{\lambda}^{-1}{\boldsymbol{M}}_{0}^{(2)}\boldsymbol{K}_{\lambda}^{-1}\boldsymbol{\nu}_{k},\quad J_{2}:=\penalty\ \frac{4a_{2}}{d^{2}}\sum_{k=1}^{d}{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}_{kk}^{2}\boldsymbol{\nu}_{k}^{\top}\boldsymbol{K}_{\lambda}^{-1}\boldsymbol{\nu}_{k}.

(271)

Here, we use
{\boldsymbol{M}}^{(2)}={\boldsymbol{b}}_{0}{\boldsymbol{b}}_{0}^{\top}+\text{diag}({\boldsymbol{b}}_{1}-\widetilde{\boldsymbol{b}}_{1}){\boldsymbol{X}}{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}{\boldsymbol{X}}^{\top}\text{diag}({\boldsymbol{b}}_{1}-\widetilde{\boldsymbol{b}}_{1})+2a_{2}^{2}{\boldsymbol{M}}_{0}^{(2)}
, and
{\boldsymbol{b}}_{0},{\boldsymbol{b}}_{1},\widetilde{\boldsymbol{b}}_{0}
, and
\widetilde{\boldsymbol{b}}_{1}
are defined in (
215
) and (
216
). Notice that
\widetilde{\boldsymbol{b}}_{1}=0
. Thus, It suffices to control
J_{1},J_{2}
and
\mathcal{R}_{\textnormal{mix}}
below. Notice that with probability
1-d^{-1}
, due to Lemmas
26
and
36
, and (
109
),

\displaystyle J_{1}\lesssim\penalty\

\displaystyle\frac{1}{d^{4}}\sum_{k=1}^{d}\boldsymbol{\nu}_{k}^{\top}\boldsymbol{K}_{\lambda}^{-1}\boldsymbol{\nu}_{k}\lesssim d^{-\frac{10}{11}}.

(272)

Similarly, we have
J_{2}\lesssim d^{-\frac{10}{11}}
as well. Next, we further decompose
\mathcal{R}_{\textnormal{mix}}
as

\displaystyle\mathcal{R}_{\textnormal{mix}}=\penalty\

\displaystyle\mathcal{R}_{\textnormal{mix}}^{(0)}+\mathcal{R}_{\textnormal{mix}}^{(1)}+\mathcal{R}_{\textnormal{mix}}^{(2)},\quad\mathcal{R}_{\textnormal{mix}}^{(0)}:=\penalty\ \frac{1}{d^{2}}\operatorname{Tr}({\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}^{2})+\sigma^{2}_{\boldsymbol{\epsilon}}{\boldsymbol{b}}_{0}^{\top}\boldsymbol{K}_{\lambda}^{-2}{\boldsymbol{b}}_{0}-\frac{2}{d^{2}}{\boldsymbol{b}}_{0}^{\top}\boldsymbol{K}_{\lambda}^{-1}\boldsymbol{\mu},

(273)

\displaystyle\mathcal{R}_{\textnormal{mix}}^{(1)}:=\penalty\

\displaystyle{\boldsymbol{b}}_{0}^{\top}\boldsymbol{K}_{\lambda}^{-1}\mathbb{E}[{\boldsymbol{f}}_{*}{\boldsymbol{f}}_{*}^{\top}|{\boldsymbol{X}}]\boldsymbol{K}_{\lambda}^{-1}{\boldsymbol{b}}_{0},

(274)

\displaystyle\mathcal{R}_{\textnormal{mix}}^{(2)}:=\penalty\

\displaystyle\operatorname{Tr}\boldsymbol{K}_{\lambda}^{-1}\text{diag}({\boldsymbol{b}}_{1}-\widetilde{\boldsymbol{b}}_{1}){\boldsymbol{X}}{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}{\boldsymbol{X}}^{\top}\text{diag}({\boldsymbol{b}}_{1}-\widetilde{\boldsymbol{b}}_{1})\boldsymbol{K}_{\lambda}^{-1}(\sigma^{2}_{\boldsymbol{\epsilon}}{\mathbf{I}}+\mathbb{E}[{\boldsymbol{f}}_{*}{\boldsymbol{f}}_{*}^{\top}|{\boldsymbol{X}}]).

(275)

Based on Assumption
2.1
and Lemmas
37
and
38
, we can verify that
|\mathcal{R}_{\textnormal{mix}}^{(0)}|\lesssim d^{-0.4}
with probability at least
1-O(d^{-1/48})
. From (
259
), we know that

\mathbb{E}[{\boldsymbol{f}}_{*}{\boldsymbol{f}}_{*}^{\top}|{\boldsymbol{X}}]=\frac{1}{d^{2}}{\boldsymbol{X}}^{(2)}{\boldsymbol{D}}_{*}{\boldsymbol{X}}^{(2)\top}
,
where
{\boldsymbol{D}}_{*}\in\mathbb{R}^{\binom{d+1}{2}\times\binom{d+1}{2}}
is a diagonal matrix with

\displaystyle({\boldsymbol{D}}_{*})_{ij,k\ell}=\begin{cases}0&\text{ if }(i,j)\not=(k,\ell),\\
2&\text{ if }i\not=j,(i,j)=(k,\ell),\\
1&\text{ if }i=j=k=\ell.\end{cases}

(276)

Hence,
{\boldsymbol{D}}_{*}\preccurlyeq 2{\mathbf{I}}
and

\mathbb{E}[{\boldsymbol{f}}_{*}{\boldsymbol{f}}_{*}^{\top}|{\boldsymbol{X}}]\preccurlyeq\frac{2}{d^{2}}{\boldsymbol{X}}^{(2)}{\boldsymbol{X}}^{(2)\top}.

(277)

Then by Lemma
37
,

|\mathcal{R}_{\textnormal{mix}}^{(1)}|\lesssim\frac{1}{d^{2}}{\boldsymbol{b}}_{0}^{\top}\boldsymbol{K}_{\lambda}^{-1}{\boldsymbol{X}}^{(2)}{\boldsymbol{X}}^{(2)\top}\boldsymbol{K}_{\lambda}^{-1}{\boldsymbol{b}}_{0}\lesssim a_{2}{\boldsymbol{b}}_{0}^{\top}\boldsymbol{K}_{\lambda}^{-1}({\boldsymbol{X}}{\boldsymbol{X}}^{\top})^{\odot 2}\boldsymbol{K}_{\lambda}^{-1}{\boldsymbol{b}}_{0}
.
Then, (
233
) allows us to get

|\mathcal{R}_{\textnormal{mix}}^{(1)}|\lesssim{\boldsymbol{b}}_{0}^{\top}\boldsymbol{K}_{\lambda}^{-1}\boldsymbol{K}_{*}^{(2)}\boldsymbol{K}_{\lambda}^{-1}{\boldsymbol{b}}_{0}+{\boldsymbol{b}}_{0}^{\top}\boldsymbol{K}_{\lambda}^{-1}{\boldsymbol{U}}{\boldsymbol{D}}{\boldsymbol{U}}^{\top}\boldsymbol{K}_{\lambda}^{-1}{\boldsymbol{b}}_{0},

where
\boldsymbol{K}_{*}^{(2)}
is defined in (
234
). Hence, Lemmas
32
and
37
imply

\displaystyle{\boldsymbol{b}}_{0}^{\top}\boldsymbol{K}_{\lambda}^{-1}\boldsymbol{K}_{*}^{(2)}\boldsymbol{K}_{\lambda}^{-1}{\boldsymbol{b}}_{0}\lesssim{\boldsymbol{b}}_{0}^{\top}\boldsymbol{K}_{\lambda}^{-2}{\boldsymbol{b}}_{0}\lesssim d^{-0.8}

(278)

with probability at least
1-O(d^{-1/48})
. Then, recall (
233
) and Lemma
38
. We can apply the Cauchy-Schwarz inequality again to get

\displaystyle|{\boldsymbol{b}}_{0}^{\top}\boldsymbol{K}_{\lambda}^{-1}{\boldsymbol{U}}{\boldsymbol{D}}{\boldsymbol{U}}^{\top}\boldsymbol{K}_{\lambda}^{-1}{\boldsymbol{b}}_{0}|

(279)

\displaystyle\leq\penalty\

\displaystyle a_{2}|{\boldsymbol{b}}_{0}^{\top}\boldsymbol{K}_{\lambda}^{-1}\mathbf{1}|\cdot\big(\operatorname{Tr}({\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}^{2})|{\boldsymbol{b}}_{0}^{\top}\boldsymbol{K}_{\lambda}^{-1}\mathbf{1}|+|{\boldsymbol{b}}_{0}^{\top}\boldsymbol{K}_{\lambda}^{-1}\boldsymbol{\mu}|\big)

(280)

\displaystyle\lesssim\penalty\

\displaystyle\frac{1}{d^{2}}\operatorname{Tr}({\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}^{2})\cdot({\boldsymbol{b}}_{0}^{\top}\boldsymbol{K}_{\lambda}^{-1}{\boldsymbol{b}}_{0})(\mathbf{1}^{\top}\boldsymbol{K}_{\lambda}^{-1}\mathbf{1})+({\boldsymbol{b}}_{0}^{\top}\boldsymbol{K}_{\lambda}^{-1}{\boldsymbol{b}}_{0})^{\frac{1}{2}}\big(\frac{1}{d^{4}}\boldsymbol{\mu}^{\top}\boldsymbol{K}_{\lambda}^{-1}\boldsymbol{\mu}\big)^{\frac{1}{2}}\lesssim\penalty\ d^{-0.4},

(281)

with probability at least
1-O(d^{-1/48})
. Lastly, because of (
109
) and (
277
), we have

\displaystyle|\mathcal{R}_{\textnormal{mix}}^{(2)}|\lesssim\penalty\

\displaystyle d\cdot\|\text{diag}({\boldsymbol{b}}_{1}-\widetilde{\boldsymbol{b}}_{1})\|^{2}\|{\boldsymbol{X}}{\boldsymbol{X}}^{\top}\|(\sigma^{2}_{\boldsymbol{\epsilon}}+\frac{2}{d^{2}}\|{\boldsymbol{X}}^{(2)}{\boldsymbol{X}}^{(2)\top}\|)\lesssim\frac{1}{d}

(282)

with probability at least
1-O(d^{-1/48})
, where we apply Lemma
34
for
\|\text{diag}({\boldsymbol{b}}_{1}-\widetilde{\boldsymbol{b}}_{1})\|
and Lemma
32
for
\|{\boldsymbol{X}}{\boldsymbol{X}}^{\top}\|
and
\|{\boldsymbol{X}}^{(2)}{\boldsymbol{X}}^{(2)\top}\|
.
∎

Lemma 43
.

Denote by

{\mathcal{V}}_{0}:=a_{2}^{2}\operatorname{Tr}\big(a_{2}{\boldsymbol{X}}^{(2)}{\boldsymbol{X}}^{(2)\top}+(\lambda+a){\mathbf{I}}\big)^{-2}{{\boldsymbol{X}}}^{(2)}{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}^{(2)}{{\boldsymbol{X}}}^{(2)\top}
.
Under the assumptions of Theorem
4
, there exist some constants
c,C>0
such that

\left|{\mathcal{V}}-{\mathcal{V}}_{0}\right|\leq Cd^{-\frac{1}{12}},

with probability at least
1-cd^{-\frac{1}{48}}
for all large
d
and
n
, and some constant
c>0
.

Proof.

Denote that
\boldsymbol{K}_{\lambda,(2)}:=(\boldsymbol{K}^{(2)}+\lambda{\mathbf{I}})
. Because of (
109
), we know that
\|\boldsymbol{K}_{\lambda,(2)}^{-1}\|\lesssim 1
and
\|\boldsymbol{K}_{\lambda}^{-1}\|\lesssim 1
. Denote by
{\mathcal{V}}^{(2)}:=2a_{2}^{2}\operatorname{Tr}\boldsymbol{K}_{\lambda,(2)}^{-1}{\boldsymbol{M}}_{0}^{(2)}\boldsymbol{K}_{\lambda,(2)}^{-1}
. We first control

\displaystyle\left|{\mathcal{V}}-{\mathcal{V}}^{(2)}\right|\lesssim\frac{a_{2}}{d^{2}}|\operatorname{Tr}(\boldsymbol{K}_{\lambda}^{-1}-\boldsymbol{K}^{-1}_{\lambda,(2)}){\boldsymbol{M}}_{0}^{(2)}\boldsymbol{K}_{\lambda}^{-1}|+\frac{a_{2}}{d^{2}}|\operatorname{Tr}\boldsymbol{K}^{-1}_{\lambda,(2)}{\boldsymbol{M}}_{0}^{(2)}(\boldsymbol{K}_{\lambda}^{-1}-\boldsymbol{K}^{-1}_{\lambda,(2)})|.

(283)

Notice that

\displaystyle\frac{a_{2}}{d^{2}}|\operatorname{Tr}(\boldsymbol{K}_{\lambda}^{-1}-\boldsymbol{K}^{-1}_{\lambda,(2)}){\boldsymbol{M}}_{0}^{(2)}\boldsymbol{K}_{\lambda}^{-1}|=\penalty\

\displaystyle\frac{a_{2}}{d^{2}}|\operatorname{Tr}\boldsymbol{K}^{-1}_{\lambda,(2)}(\boldsymbol{K}^{(2)}-\boldsymbol{K})\boldsymbol{K}_{\lambda}^{-1}{\boldsymbol{M}}_{0}^{(2)}\boldsymbol{K}_{\lambda}^{-1}|

(284)

\displaystyle\lesssim\penalty\

\displaystyle\frac{1}{d^{2}}\|\boldsymbol{K}^{(2)}-\boldsymbol{K}\|\cdot|\operatorname{Tr}\boldsymbol{K}_{\lambda}^{-1}(a_{2}{\boldsymbol{M}}_{0}^{(2)})\boldsymbol{K}_{\lambda}^{-1}|

(285)

\displaystyle\lesssim\penalty\

\displaystyle d^{-\frac{1}{12}}\cdot\frac{n}{d^{2}}\left\|\boldsymbol{K}_{\lambda}^{-1}(a_{2}{\boldsymbol{X}}{\boldsymbol{X}}^{\top})^{\odot 2}\boldsymbol{K}_{\lambda}^{-1}\right\|\lesssim d^{-\frac{1}{12}},

(286)

with probability at least
1-O(d^{-1/2})
, where we apply Lemma
26
and
Theorem
1
. We can get a similar argument for the second term:

\displaystyle\frac{a_{2}}{d^{2}}|\operatorname{Tr}\boldsymbol{K}^{-1}_{\lambda,(2)}{\boldsymbol{M}}_{0}^{(2)}(\boldsymbol{K}_{\lambda}^{-1}-\boldsymbol{K}^{-1}_{\lambda,(2)})|\leq\penalty\

\displaystyle\frac{a_{2}}{d^{2}}|\operatorname{Tr}\boldsymbol{K}^{-1}_{\lambda,(2)}{\boldsymbol{M}}_{0}^{(2)}\boldsymbol{K}^{-1}_{\lambda,(2)}(\boldsymbol{K}-\boldsymbol{K}^{(2)})\boldsymbol{K}_{\lambda}^{-1}|

(287)

\displaystyle\lesssim\penalty\

\displaystyle d^{-\frac{1}{12}}.

(288)

Next, we approximate
{\mathcal{V}}^{(2)}
by
{\mathcal{V}}_{0}
. Let us denote by
{\mathcal{V}}_{0}^{(2)}:=a_{2}^{2}\operatorname{Tr}\boldsymbol{K}_{\lambda,(2)}^{-2}{\boldsymbol{X}}^{(2)}{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}^{(2)}{\boldsymbol{X}}^{(2)\top}
. From Lemma
36
, we know that

{\mathcal{V}}^{(2)}={\mathcal{V}}_{0}^{(2)}-\sum_{k=1}^{d}{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}_{kk}^{2}a_{2}^{2}\boldsymbol{\nu}_{k}^{\top}\boldsymbol{K}_{\lambda,(2)}^{-2}\boldsymbol{\nu}_{k}
, where the second term on the right-hand side satisfies

\displaystyle\left|\sum_{k=1}^{d}{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}_{kk}^{2}a_{2}^{2}\boldsymbol{\nu}_{k}^{\top}\boldsymbol{K}_{\lambda,(2)}^{-2}\boldsymbol{\nu}_{k}\right|\lesssim\penalty\

\displaystyle\frac{1}{d^{3}}\max_{k\in[d]}\boldsymbol{\nu}_{k}^{\top}\boldsymbol{K}_{\lambda,(2)}^{-2}\boldsymbol{\nu}_{k}\lesssim\frac{1}{d^{3}}\max_{k\in[d]}\|\boldsymbol{\nu}_{k}\|^{2}\lesssim d^{-\frac{10}{11}},

(289)

with probability at least
1-d^{-1}
. Thus, it suffices to control the difference between
{\mathcal{V}}_{0}^{(2)}
and
{\mathcal{V}}_{0}
. Notice that

{\mathcal{V}}_{0}^{(2)}=a_{2}^{2}\operatorname{Tr}\big(a_{0}\mathbf{1}\mathbf{1}^{\top}+\boldsymbol{K}_{*}\big)^{-2}{\boldsymbol{X}}^{(2)}{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}^{(2)}{\boldsymbol{X}}^{(2)\top}
, where we define

\boldsymbol{K}_{*}:=a_{2}{\boldsymbol{X}}^{(2)}{\boldsymbol{X}}^{(2)\top}+(\lambda+a){\mathbf{I}}.

(290)

Analogously to the proof of Lemma
37
, the Sherman-Morrison-Woodbury formula implies

\big(a_{0}\mathbf{1}\mathbf{1}^{\top}+\boldsymbol{K}_{*}\big)^{-1}=\boldsymbol{K}_{*}^{-1}-a_{0}\frac{\boldsymbol{K}_{*}^{-1}\mathbf{1}\mathbf{1}^{\top}\boldsymbol{K}_{*}^{-1}}{1+a_{0}\mathbf{1}^{\top}\boldsymbol{K}_{*}^{-1}\mathbf{1}}.
Thus, we have

\displaystyle{\mathcal{V}}_{0}^{(2)}={\mathcal{V}}_{0}

(291)

\displaystyle+\frac{a_{2}^{2}(a_{0}\mathbf{1}^{\top}\boldsymbol{K}_{*}^{-2}\mathbf{1})\cdot(a_{0}\mathbf{1}^{\top}\boldsymbol{K}_{*}^{-1}{\boldsymbol{X}}^{(2)}{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}^{(2)}{\boldsymbol{X}}^{(2)\top}\boldsymbol{K}_{*}^{-1}\mathbf{1})}{(1+a_{0}\mathbf{1}^{\top}\boldsymbol{K}_{*}^{-1}\mathbf{1})^{2}}-\frac{2a_{2}^{2}\cdot a_{0}\mathbf{1}^{\top}\boldsymbol{K}_{*}^{-1}{\boldsymbol{X}}^{(2)}{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}^{(2)}{\boldsymbol{X}}^{(2)\top}\boldsymbol{K}_{*}^{-2}\mathbf{1}}{1+a_{0}\mathbf{1}^{\top}\boldsymbol{K}_{*}^{-1}\mathbf{1}}.

(292)

Hence, we only need to control the last two terms on the right-hand side of the above equation.
By Assumption
2.3.1
and Lemma
32
, we know

cd^{-1}{\mathbf{I}}\preccurlyeq\boldsymbol{K}_{*}^{-1}\preccurlyeq C{\mathbf{I}}
,
with probability at least
1-O(d^{-1/48})
, for some constants
c,C>0
. And Lemma
26
indicates that

a_{2}\boldsymbol{K}_{*}^{-1/2}{\boldsymbol{X}}^{(2)}{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}^{(2)}{\boldsymbol{X}}^{(2)\top}\boldsymbol{K}_{*}^{-1/2}\preccurlyeq C\cdot a_{2}\boldsymbol{K}_{*}^{-1/2}({\boldsymbol{X}}{\boldsymbol{X}}^{\top})^{\odot 2}\boldsymbol{K}_{*}^{-1/2}\preccurlyeq C.

Therefore,

\displaystyle\frac{a_{2}^{2}(a_{0}\mathbf{1}^{\top}\boldsymbol{K}_{*}^{-2}\mathbf{1})\cdot(a_{0}\mathbf{1}^{\top}\boldsymbol{K}_{*}^{-1}{\boldsymbol{X}}^{(2)}{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}^{(2)}{\boldsymbol{X}}^{(2)\top}\boldsymbol{K}_{*}^{-1}\mathbf{1})}{(1+a_{0}\mathbf{1}^{\top}\boldsymbol{K}_{*}^{-1}\mathbf{1})^{2}}

(293)

\displaystyle=\penalty\

\displaystyle\frac{a_{0}\mathbf{1}^{\top}\boldsymbol{K}_{*}^{-1}\mathbf{1}}{1+a_{0}\mathbf{1}^{\top}\boldsymbol{K}_{*}^{-1}\mathbf{1}}\frac{a_{2}\cdot(a_{0}\mathbf{1}^{\top}\boldsymbol{K}_{*}^{-1}(a_{2}{\boldsymbol{X}}^{(2)}{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}^{(2)}{\boldsymbol{X}}^{(2)\top})\boldsymbol{K}_{*}^{-1}\mathbf{1})}{1+a_{0}\mathbf{1}^{\top}\boldsymbol{K}_{*}^{-1}\mathbf{1}}

(294)

\displaystyle\leq\penalty\

\displaystyle Ca_{2}\cdot\frac{a_{0}\mathbf{1}^{\top}\boldsymbol{K}_{*}^{-1}\mathbf{1}}{{1+a_{0}\mathbf{1}^{\top}\boldsymbol{K}_{*}^{-1}\mathbf{1}}}\lesssim\frac{1}{d^{2}}.

(295)

Similarly, we have

\frac{2a_{2}^{2}\cdot a_{0}\mathbf{1}^{\top}\boldsymbol{K}_{*}^{-1}{\boldsymbol{X}}^{(2)}{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}^{(2)}{\boldsymbol{X}}^{(2)\top}\boldsymbol{K}_{*}^{-2}\mathbf{1}}{1+a_{0}\mathbf{1}^{\top}\boldsymbol{K}_{*}^{-1}\mathbf{1}}\leq 2Ca_{2}\frac{a_{0}\mathbf{1}^{\top}\boldsymbol{K}_{*}^{-1}\mathbf{1}}{1+a_{0}\mathbf{1}^{\top}\boldsymbol{K}_{*}^{-1}\mathbf{1}}\lesssim\frac{1}{d^{2}}
. Hence, we complete the proof of this lemma.
∎

Lemma 44
.

Denote

\mathcal{B}_{0}:=\frac{2}{d^{2}}\operatorname{Tr}{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}^{(2)}+\frac{2a_{2}^{2}}{d^{2}}\operatorname{Tr}\boldsymbol{K}_{*}^{-1}{\boldsymbol{X}}^{(2)}{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}^{(2)}{\boldsymbol{X}}^{(2)\top}\boldsymbol{K}_{*}^{-1}({\boldsymbol{X}}{\boldsymbol{X}}^{\top})^{\odot 2}-\frac{4a_{2}}{d^{2}}\operatorname{Tr}\boldsymbol{K}_{*}^{-1}{\boldsymbol{X}}^{(2)}{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}^{(2)}{\boldsymbol{X}}^{(2)\top}

where
\boldsymbol{K}_{*}
is defined in (
290
). Under the assumptions of Theorem
1
, there exist some constants
c,C>0
such that

\left|\mathcal{B}-\mathcal{B}_{0}\right|\leq Cd^{-\frac{1}{12}},

with probability at least
1-cd^{-\frac{1}{48}}
.

Proof.

Recall
\boldsymbol{K}_{\lambda,(2)}=(\boldsymbol{K}^{(2)}+\lambda{\mathbf{I}})
and the definition of
\mathcal{B}
in Lemma
42
. Define

\mathcal{B}^{(2)}:=\frac{2}{d^{2}}(\operatorname{Tr}{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}})^{2}+\frac{4a_{2}^{2}}{d^{2}}\operatorname{Tr}\boldsymbol{K}_{\lambda,(2)}^{-1}{\boldsymbol{M}}_{0}^{(2)}\boldsymbol{K}_{\lambda,(2)}^{-1}({\boldsymbol{X}}{\boldsymbol{X}}^{\top})^{\odot 2}-\frac{4a_{2}}{d^{2}}\operatorname{Tr}\boldsymbol{K}_{\lambda,(2)}^{-1}{\boldsymbol{X}}^{(2)}{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}^{(2)}{\boldsymbol{X}}^{(2)\top}.

Then, following the same analysis as (
283
), (
286
), and (
288
), we can obtain that

\displaystyle|\mathcal{B}^{(2)}-\mathcal{B}|\lesssim\penalty\

\displaystyle\frac{a_{2}^{2}}{d^{2}}|\operatorname{Tr}(\boldsymbol{K}_{\lambda}^{-1}-\boldsymbol{K}_{\lambda,(2)}^{-1}){\boldsymbol{M}}_{0}^{(2)}\boldsymbol{K}_{\lambda}^{-1}({\boldsymbol{X}}{\boldsymbol{X}}^{\top})^{\odot 2}|

(296)

\displaystyle+\frac{a_{2}^{2}}{d^{2}}|\operatorname{Tr}\boldsymbol{K}_{\lambda,(2)}^{-1}{\boldsymbol{M}}_{0}^{(2)}(\boldsymbol{K}_{\lambda}^{-1}-\boldsymbol{K}_{\lambda,(2)}^{-1})({\boldsymbol{X}}{\boldsymbol{X}}^{\top})^{\odot 2}|

(297)

\displaystyle+\frac{a_{2}}{d^{2}}|\operatorname{Tr}{\boldsymbol{X}}^{(2)}{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}^{(2)}{\boldsymbol{X}}^{(2)\top}(\boldsymbol{K}_{\lambda}^{-1}-\boldsymbol{K}_{\lambda,(2)}^{-1})|

(298)

\displaystyle\lesssim\penalty\

\displaystyle\|\boldsymbol{K}-\boldsymbol{K}^{(2)}\|\cdot\big(a_{2}^{2}\|\boldsymbol{K}_{\lambda}^{-1}{\boldsymbol{M}}_{0}^{(2)}\boldsymbol{K}_{\lambda,(2)}^{-1}({\boldsymbol{X}}{\boldsymbol{X}}^{\top})^{\odot 2}\boldsymbol{K}_{\lambda}^{-1}\|

(299)

\displaystyle+a_{2}^{2}\|\boldsymbol{K}_{\lambda,(2)}^{-1}{\boldsymbol{M}}_{0}^{(2)}\boldsymbol{K}_{\lambda,(2)}^{-1}({\boldsymbol{X}}{\boldsymbol{X}}^{\top})^{\odot 2}\boldsymbol{K}_{\lambda}^{-1}\|+a_{2}\|\boldsymbol{K}_{\lambda}^{-1}{\boldsymbol{X}}^{(2)}{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}^{(2)}{\boldsymbol{X}}^{(2)\top}\boldsymbol{K}_{\lambda,(2)}^{-1}\|\big)

(300)

\displaystyle\lesssim\penalty\

\displaystyle d^{-\frac{1}{12}},

(301)

with probability at least
1-O(d^{-1/2})
,
where we apply Theorem
1
and Lemma
26
. Next, we apply Lemma
36
and define

\displaystyle\mathcal{B}^{(2)}=\penalty\

\displaystyle\mathcal{B}_{0}^{(2)}-{\boldsymbol{\Delta}}_{\mathcal{B}},

(302)

\displaystyle\mathcal{B}_{0}^{(2)}:=\penalty\

\displaystyle\frac{2}{d^{2}}\operatorname{Tr}{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}^{(2)}+\frac{2a_{2}^{2}}{d^{2}}\operatorname{Tr}\boldsymbol{K}_{\lambda,(2)}^{-1}{\boldsymbol{X}}^{(2)}{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}^{(2)}{\boldsymbol{X}}^{(2)\top}\boldsymbol{K}_{\lambda,(2)}^{-1}({\boldsymbol{X}}{\boldsymbol{X}}^{\top})^{\odot 2}

(303)

\displaystyle-\frac{4a_{2}}{d^{2}}\operatorname{Tr}\boldsymbol{K}_{\lambda,(2)}^{-1}{\boldsymbol{X}}^{(2)}{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}^{(2)}{\boldsymbol{X}}^{(2)\top},

(304)

\displaystyle{\boldsymbol{\Delta}}_{\mathcal{B}}:=\penalty\

\displaystyle\frac{4\operatorname{Tr}({\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}^{2})}{d^{2}}+\frac{a_{2}^{2}}{d^{2}}\sum_{k=1}^{d}{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}_{kk}^{2}\boldsymbol{\nu}_{k}^{\top}\boldsymbol{K}_{\lambda,(2)}^{-1}({\boldsymbol{X}}{\boldsymbol{X}}^{\top})^{\odot 2}\boldsymbol{K}_{\lambda,(2)}^{-1}\boldsymbol{\nu}_{k}.

(305)

Then, analogously to (
289
), we can have

|{\boldsymbol{\Delta}}_{\mathcal{B}}|\lesssim\frac{4\operatorname{Tr}({\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}^{2})}{d^{2}}+\frac{a_{2}}{d^{2}}\sum_{k=1}^{d}\boldsymbol{\nu}_{k}^{\top}\boldsymbol{K}_{\lambda,(2)}^{-1}\boldsymbol{\nu}_{k}\lesssim d^{-\frac{10}{11}}
.
with probability at least
1-O(d^{-1})
. Finally, the difference between
\mathcal{B}_{0}^{(2)}
and
\mathcal{B}_{0}
can be controlled similar as the bound of
|{\mathcal{V}}_{0}-{\mathcal{V}}_{0}^{(2)}|
from the proof of Lemma
43
. We ignore the details for the last step here.
∎

Proof of Theorem
4

Based on all above Lemmas
41
,
42
,
43
, and
44
, we have already known that
|\mathcal{R}_{0}-\mathcal{R}(\lambda)|\to 0
in probability, as
d^{2}/(2n)\to\alpha
and
d\to\infty
, where
\mathcal{R}_{0}:=\sigma_{\boldsymbol{\epsilon}}^{2}{\mathcal{V}}_{0}+\mathcal{B}_{0}
.
Here
{\mathcal{V}}_{0}
and
\mathcal{B}_{0}
are defined in Lemmas
43
, and
44
, respectively. Hence, to prove Theorem
4
, it suffices to analyze the asymptotic behavior of
\mathcal{R}_{0}
, as
d^{2}/(2n)\to\alpha
and
d\to\infty
. Recall the definition of
\boldsymbol{K}_{*}
in (
290
) and
({\boldsymbol{X}}{\boldsymbol{X}}^{\top})^{\odot 2}={\boldsymbol{X}}^{(2)}{\boldsymbol{X}}^{(2)\top}
. As
d\to\infty
and
d^{2}/(2n)\to\alpha\in(0,\infty)
, it is easy to check that

\displaystyle\mathcal{B}_{0}=\penalty\

\displaystyle\frac{2}{d^{2}}\operatorname{Tr}{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}^{(2)}+\frac{2a_{2}^{2}}{d^{2}}\operatorname{Tr}\boldsymbol{K}_{*}^{-1}{\boldsymbol{X}}^{(2)}{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}^{(2)}{\boldsymbol{X}}^{(2)\top}\boldsymbol{K}_{*}^{-1}({\boldsymbol{X}}{\boldsymbol{X}}^{\top})^{\odot 2}-\frac{4a_{2}}{d^{2}}\operatorname{Tr}\boldsymbol{K}_{*}^{-1}{\boldsymbol{X}}^{(2)}{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}^{(2)}{\boldsymbol{X}}^{(2)\top}

(306)

\displaystyle=\penalty\

\displaystyle\frac{2}{d^{2}}\operatorname{Tr}\big({\mathbf{I}}-a_{2}{\boldsymbol{X}}^{(2)\top}\boldsymbol{K}_{*}^{-1}{\boldsymbol{X}}^{(2)}\big){\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}^{(2)}\big({\mathbf{I}}-a_{2}{\boldsymbol{X}}^{(2)\top}\boldsymbol{K}_{*}^{-1}{\boldsymbol{X}}^{(2)}\big)

(307)

\displaystyle=\penalty\

\displaystyle\frac{2(a+\lambda)^{2}}{d^{2}}\operatorname{Tr}\big((a+\lambda){\mathbf{I}}+a_{2}{\boldsymbol{X}}^{(2)\top}{\boldsymbol{X}}^{(2)}\big)^{-1}{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}^{(2)}\big((a+\lambda){\mathbf{I}}+a_{2}{\boldsymbol{X}}^{(2)\top}{\boldsymbol{X}}^{(2)}\big)^{-1}

(308)

\displaystyle=\penalty\

\displaystyle\frac{2(a_{*}+\lambda)^{2}}{d^{2}}\operatorname{Tr}\big((a+\lambda){\mathbf{I}}+a_{2}\overline{{\boldsymbol{X}}}^{(2)\top}\overline{{\boldsymbol{X}}}^{(2)}\big)^{-1}{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}^{(2)}\big((a+\lambda){\mathbf{I}}+a_{2}\overline{{\boldsymbol{X}}}^{(2)\top}\overline{{\boldsymbol{X}}}^{(2)}\big)^{-1}+o(1),

(309)

and

\displaystyle{\mathcal{V}}_{0}=\penalty\

\displaystyle a_{2}^{2}\operatorname{Tr}\big(a_{2}{\boldsymbol{X}}^{(2)}{\boldsymbol{X}}^{(2)\top}+(\lambda+a){\mathbf{I}}\big)^{-2}{{\boldsymbol{X}}}^{(2)}{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}^{(2)}{{\boldsymbol{X}}}^{(2)\top}

(310)

\displaystyle=\penalty\

\displaystyle a_{2}\operatorname{Tr}\big((a+\lambda){\mathbf{I}}+a_{2}{\boldsymbol{X}}^{(2)\top}{\boldsymbol{X}}^{(2)}\big)^{-1}{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}^{(2)}\big((a+\lambda){\mathbf{I}}+a_{2}{\boldsymbol{X}}^{(2)\top}{\boldsymbol{X}}^{(2)}\big)^{-1}\big(a_{2}{\boldsymbol{X}}^{(2)\top}{\boldsymbol{X}}^{(2)}\big)

(311)

\displaystyle=\penalty\

\displaystyle a_{2}\operatorname{Tr}\big((a+\lambda){\mathbf{I}}+a_{2}{\boldsymbol{X}}^{(2)\top}{\boldsymbol{X}}^{(2)}\big)^{-1}{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}^{(2)}

(312)

\displaystyle-a_{2}(a+\lambda)\operatorname{Tr}\big((a+\lambda){\mathbf{I}}+a_{2}{\boldsymbol{X}}^{(2)\top}{\boldsymbol{X}}^{(2)}\big)^{-1}{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}^{(2)}\big((a+\lambda){\mathbf{I}}+a_{2}{\boldsymbol{X}}^{(2)\top}{\boldsymbol{X}}^{(2)}\big)^{-1}

(313)

\displaystyle=\penalty\

\displaystyle a_{2}\operatorname{Tr}\big((a_{*}+\lambda){\mathbf{I}}+a_{2}\overline{{\boldsymbol{X}}}^{(2)\top}\overline{{\boldsymbol{X}}}^{(2)}\big)^{-1}{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}^{(2)}

(314)

\displaystyle-a_{2}(a_{*}+\lambda)\operatorname{Tr}\big((a+\lambda){\mathbf{I}}+a_{2}\overline{{\boldsymbol{X}}}^{(2)\top}\overline{{\boldsymbol{X}}}^{(2)}\big)^{-1}{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}^{(2)}\big((a_{*}+\lambda){\mathbf{I}}+a_{2}\overline{{\boldsymbol{X}}}^{(2)\top}\overline{{\boldsymbol{X}}}^{(2)}\big)^{-1}+o(1),

(315)

where
{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}^{(2)}
is the population covariance matrix of
{\boldsymbol{x}}_{i}^{(2)}
defined in (
17
). Recall that
{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}^{(2)}
has a limiting spectral distribution
\mu_{{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}^{(2)}}
as
d^{2}/(2n)\to\alpha
and
n\to\infty
. Therefore, we can apply Lemma
31
to conclude this theorem.

E.3
Proof of Theorem
7

Following the same notions in Section
E.1.5
, in the setting of Theorem

, we know that

\displaystyle\mathcal{R}(\lambda)=\penalty\

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{{\boldsymbol{x}}}[|{\boldsymbol{f}}_{*}({\boldsymbol{x}})|^{2}]+{\boldsymbol{f}}_{*}^{\top}(\boldsymbol{K}+\lambda{\mathbf{I}})^{-1}{\boldsymbol{M}}(\boldsymbol{K}+\lambda{\mathbf{I}})^{-1}{\boldsymbol{f}}_{*}

(316)

\displaystyle+\sigma^{2}_{\boldsymbol{\epsilon}}\operatorname{Tr}(\boldsymbol{K}+\lambda{\mathbf{I}})^{-1}{\boldsymbol{M}}(\boldsymbol{K}+\lambda{\mathbf{I}})^{-1}-2{\boldsymbol{v}}^{\top}(\boldsymbol{K}+\lambda{\mathbf{I}})^{-1}{\boldsymbol{f}}_{*}.

(317)

Let us redefine that

\displaystyle{\boldsymbol{v}}^{(2)}:=\penalty\

\displaystyle\frac{1}{d}\operatorname{Tr}({\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}^{2}){\boldsymbol{b}}_{0}+\frac{2a_{2}}{d}{\boldsymbol{v}}^{(2)}_{0},\quad{\boldsymbol{v}}^{(2)}_{0}:=[{\boldsymbol{x}}_{1}^{\top}{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}^{3}{\boldsymbol{x}}_{1},\ldots,{\boldsymbol{x}}_{n}^{\top}{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}^{3}{\boldsymbol{x}}_{n}]^{\top}.

(318)

In the following, we first provide the approximations of
{\boldsymbol{v}}
in terms of
{\boldsymbol{v}}^{(2)}
. And analogously to Lemma
41
, in the following, we will use

\displaystyle\widetilde{}\mathcal{R}(\lambda)=\penalty\

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{{\boldsymbol{x}}}[|{\boldsymbol{f}}_{*}({\boldsymbol{x}})|^{2}]+{\boldsymbol{f}}_{*}^{\top}(\boldsymbol{K}+\lambda{\mathbf{I}})^{-1}{\boldsymbol{M}}^{(2)}(\boldsymbol{K}+\lambda{\mathbf{I}})^{-1}{\boldsymbol{f}}_{*}

(319)

\displaystyle+\sigma^{2}_{\boldsymbol{\epsilon}}\operatorname{Tr}(\boldsymbol{K}+\lambda{\mathbf{I}})^{-1}{\boldsymbol{M}}^{(2)}(\boldsymbol{K}+\lambda{\mathbf{I}})^{-1}-2{\boldsymbol{v}}^{(2)\top}(\boldsymbol{K}+\lambda{\mathbf{I}})^{-1}{\boldsymbol{f}}_{*}

(320)

to approximate generalization error
\mathcal{R}(\lambda)
.
Notice that, under the assumptions of Theorem
7
,
{\boldsymbol{f}}_{*}=\frac{1}{d}\boldsymbol{\mu}
where
\boldsymbol{\mu}
is defined by (
230
), and

{\boldsymbol{M}}^{(2)}={\boldsymbol{b}}_{0}{\boldsymbol{b}}_{0}^{\top}+\text{diag}({\boldsymbol{b}}_{1}-\widetilde{\boldsymbol{b}}_{1}){\boldsymbol{X}}{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}{\boldsymbol{X}}^{\top}\text{diag}({\boldsymbol{b}}_{1}-\widetilde{\boldsymbol{b}}_{1})+2a_{2}^{2}{\boldsymbol{M}}_{0}^{(2)}.

Lemma 45
.

Under the same assumptions as Theorem
1
, we have that

\|{\boldsymbol{v}}-{\boldsymbol{v}}^{(2)}\|\leq\penalty\ \frac{c}{d^{2}}
,
with probability at least
1-O(d^{-1})
for some constant
c>0
.

Proof.

For any
i\in[n]
, by the definition of
f_{*}({\boldsymbol{x}})
and (
204
), we have

\displaystyle{\boldsymbol{v}}_{i}=\mathbb{E}_{{\boldsymbol{x}}}[K({\boldsymbol{x}},{\boldsymbol{x}}_{i})f_{*}({\boldsymbol{x}})]

\displaystyle=\sum_{k=0}^{8}b_{k,i}\mathbb{E}_{{\boldsymbol{x}}}[{\boldsymbol{T}}_{i}^{(k)}f_{*}({\boldsymbol{x}})]+\mathbb{E}_{{\boldsymbol{x}}}\left[\frac{f^{(9)}(\zeta_{i})}{9!d^{9}}f_{*}({\boldsymbol{x}})\langle{\boldsymbol{x}}_{i},{\boldsymbol{x}}\rangle^{9}\right]

(321)

\displaystyle=\frac{b_{0,i}}{d}\operatorname{Tr}({\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}^{2})+\frac{2b_{2,i}}{d}{\boldsymbol{x}}_{i}^{\top}{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}^{3}{\boldsymbol{x}}_{i}+\mathbb{E}_{{\boldsymbol{x}}}\left[\frac{f^{(9)}(\zeta_{i})}{9!d^{9}}f_{*}({\boldsymbol{x}})\langle{\boldsymbol{x}}_{i},{\boldsymbol{x}}\rangle^{9}\right]

(322)

where in the second line we applied Lemmas
20
and
33
. Notice that

\displaystyle 0<{\boldsymbol{x}}_{i}^{\top}{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}^{3}{\boldsymbol{x}}_{i}={\boldsymbol{w}}_{i}^{\top}{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}^{2}{\boldsymbol{w}}_{i}\leq\left\|{\boldsymbol{w}}_{i}\right\|^{2}\left\|{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}\right\|^{2}\lesssim d^{1+\frac{1}{15}},

(323)

with probability at least
1-d^{-1}
for all
i\in[n]
,
where we applied (
55
).
Therefore,

\displaystyle\|{\boldsymbol{v}}-{\boldsymbol{v}}^{(2)}\|\leq\penalty\

\displaystyle\frac{2}{d}\|{\boldsymbol{v}}_{0}^{(2)}\|\cdot\max_{i\in[n]}|a_{2}-b_{2,i}|+\frac{C}{d^{9}}\mathbb{E}_{{\boldsymbol{x}}}[\|({\boldsymbol{X}}{\boldsymbol{x}})^{\odot 9}f_{*}({\boldsymbol{x}})\|]

(324)

\displaystyle\lesssim\penalty\

\displaystyle\frac{1}{d^{4.4}}\|{\boldsymbol{v}}_{0}^{(2)}\|+\frac{1}{d^{9}}\cdot\mathbb{E}[\|({\boldsymbol{X}}{\boldsymbol{x}})^{\odot 9}\|^{2}]^{1/2}\mathbb{E}[f_{*}({\boldsymbol{x}})^{2}]^{1/2}

(325)

\displaystyle\lesssim\penalty\

\displaystyle\frac{\sqrt{n}}{d^{4.4}}\max_{i\in[n]}{\boldsymbol{x}}_{i}^{\top}{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}^{3}{\boldsymbol{x}}_{i}+\frac{\sqrt{n}}{d^{9}}\max_{i\in[n]}\left\|{\boldsymbol{w}}_{i}\right\|^{9}\lesssim d^{-2.3},

(326)

with probability at least
1-O(d^{-1})
, where we utilize (
323
), (
55
), Lemma
34
, and the definition of
f_{*}
. This completes the proof of the lemma.
∎

Lemma 46
.

Under the same assumptions as Theorem
7
, for any
\lambda\geq 0
, we have that

|\mathcal{R}(\lambda)-\widetilde{}\mathcal{R}(\lambda)|\lesssim d^{{-}\frac{1}{4}}
,
with probability
1-O(d^{-1/48})
, where
\mathcal{R}(\lambda)
is defined by (
316
).

Proof.

Since
{\boldsymbol{f}}_{*}=\frac{1}{d}\boldsymbol{\mu}
, (
235
) implies that
\left\|{\boldsymbol{f}}_{*}\right\|\lesssim d
with probability at least
1-O(d^{-1})
. Then, applying Lemmas
35
and
45
, we can get

\displaystyle\Big|\widetilde{}\mathcal{R}(\lambda)-\mathcal{R}(\lambda)\Big|\leq\penalty\

\displaystyle\Big|{\boldsymbol{f}}_{*}^{\top}\boldsymbol{K}_{\lambda}^{-1}({\boldsymbol{M}}^{(2)}-{\boldsymbol{M}})\boldsymbol{K}_{\lambda}^{-1}{\boldsymbol{f}}_{*}\Big|+2\Big|{\boldsymbol{f}}_{*}^{\top}\boldsymbol{K}_{\lambda}^{-1}({\boldsymbol{v}}^{(2)}-{\boldsymbol{v}})\Big|

(327)

\displaystyle+\sigma^{2}_{\boldsymbol{\epsilon}}\Big|\operatorname{Tr}\boldsymbol{K}_{\lambda}^{-1}({\boldsymbol{M}}^{(2)}-{\boldsymbol{M}})\boldsymbol{K}_{\lambda}^{-1}\Big|

(328)

\displaystyle\leq\penalty\

\displaystyle(n\sigma^{2}_{\boldsymbol{\epsilon}}+\|{\boldsymbol{f}}_{*}\|^{2})\|\boldsymbol{K}_{\lambda}^{-1}\|^{2}\cdot\|{\boldsymbol{M}}^{(2)}-{\boldsymbol{M}}\|+2\|{\boldsymbol{f}}_{*}\|\cdot\|\boldsymbol{K}_{\lambda}^{-1}\|\cdot\|{\boldsymbol{v}}^{(2)}-{\boldsymbol{v}}\|\lesssim d^{\frac{-1}{4}},

(329)

with probability at least
1-O(d^{-1/48})
, where in the last line, we also utilize (
109
).
∎

Notice that
\widetilde{}\mathcal{R}(\lambda)
defined in (
319
) can be further decomposed by

\widetilde{}\mathcal{R}(\lambda)=\sigma_{\varepsilon}^{2}{\mathcal{V}}+\mathcal{R}_{1}+\mathcal{R}_{2}+\mathcal{R}_{\textnormal{mix}},

(330)

where
{\mathcal{V}}
is defined in Lemma
42
, and we redefine the terms:

\displaystyle\mathcal{R}_{1}:=\penalty\

\displaystyle\big(d^{-1}\operatorname{Tr}({\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}^{2})-(a_{2}\boldsymbol{\mu}+a_{0}\mathbf{1})^{\top}\boldsymbol{K}_{\lambda}^{-1}{\boldsymbol{f}}_{*})^{2}

(331)

\displaystyle\mathcal{R}_{2}:=\penalty\

\displaystyle\frac{2}{d^{2}}\operatorname{Tr}({\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}^{4})+2a_{2}^{2}{\boldsymbol{f}}_{*}^{\top}\boldsymbol{K}_{\lambda}^{-1}({\boldsymbol{X}}{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}{\boldsymbol{X}}^{\top})^{\odot 2}\boldsymbol{K}_{\lambda}^{-1}{\boldsymbol{f}}_{*}-\frac{4a_{2}}{d}{\boldsymbol{v}}_{0}^{(2)}\boldsymbol{K}_{\lambda}^{-1}{\boldsymbol{f}}_{*}

(332)

\displaystyle\mathcal{R}_{\textnormal{mix}}:=\penalty\

\displaystyle{\boldsymbol{f}}_{*}^{\top}\boldsymbol{K}_{\lambda}^{-1}({\boldsymbol{b}}_{0}{\boldsymbol{b}}_{0}^{\top}-\widetilde{\boldsymbol{b}}\widetilde{\boldsymbol{b}}_{0}^{\top})\boldsymbol{K}_{\lambda}^{-1}{\boldsymbol{f}}_{*}

(333)

\displaystyle+{\boldsymbol{f}}_{*}^{\top}\boldsymbol{K}_{\lambda}^{-1}\text{diag}({\boldsymbol{b}}_{1}-\widetilde{\boldsymbol{b}}_{1}){\boldsymbol{X}}{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}{\boldsymbol{X}}^{\top}\text{diag}({\boldsymbol{b}}_{1}-\widetilde{\boldsymbol{b}}_{1})\boldsymbol{K}_{\lambda}^{-1}{\boldsymbol{f}}_{*}-2\frac{\operatorname{Tr}({\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}^{2})}{d}({\boldsymbol{b}}_{0}-\widetilde{\boldsymbol{b}})^{\top}\boldsymbol{K}_{\lambda}^{-1}{\boldsymbol{f}}_{*}

(334)

\displaystyle+\sigma^{2}_{\boldsymbol{\epsilon}}\operatorname{Tr}\boldsymbol{K}_{\lambda}^{-1}{\boldsymbol{b}}_{0}{\boldsymbol{b}}_{0}^{\top}\boldsymbol{K}_{\lambda}^{-1}+\sigma^{2}_{\boldsymbol{\epsilon}}\operatorname{Tr}\boldsymbol{K}_{\lambda}^{-1}\text{diag}({\boldsymbol{b}}_{1}-\widetilde{\boldsymbol{b}}_{1}){\boldsymbol{X}}{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}{\boldsymbol{X}}^{\top}\text{diag}({\boldsymbol{b}}_{1}-\widetilde{\boldsymbol{b}}_{1})\boldsymbol{K}_{\lambda}^{-1}.

(335)

Here, we denote

\displaystyle\widetilde{\boldsymbol{b}}:=a_{2}\boldsymbol{\mu}+a_{0}\mathbf{1},

(336)

and
{\boldsymbol{b}}_{0},{\boldsymbol{b}}_{1},

\widetilde{\boldsymbol{b}}_{1}
are defined in Lemma
34
.
The analysis of
{\mathcal{V}}
is the same as the proof of Theorem
4
. Now recall some notations introduced in Section
E.1.6
. We denote by

\displaystyle{\boldsymbol{U}}=\penalty\

\displaystyle[\mathbf{1},\boldsymbol{\mu}]\in\mathbb{R}^{n\times 2}

(337)

\displaystyle{\boldsymbol{D}}:=\penalty\

\displaystyle\begin{pmatrix}a_{0}-a_{2}\operatorname{Tr}({\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}^{2})&a_{2}\\
a_{2}&0\end{pmatrix}

(338)

Then, we have

\boldsymbol{K}_{\lambda}={\boldsymbol{U}}{\boldsymbol{D}}{\boldsymbol{U}}^{\top}+\boldsymbol{K}_{*}
,
where
\boldsymbol{K}_{*}
satisfies

c{\mathbf{I}}\preccurlyeq\boldsymbol{K}_{*}\preccurlyeq Cd^{\frac{1}{6}}{\mathbf{I}},

(339)

with probability at least
1-O(d^{-\frac{1}{48}})
, for some constants
c,C>0
. This is based on Theorem
1
and Lemma
32
. Then, applying the Sherman-Morrison-Woodbury formula again, we can derive that

\displaystyle{\boldsymbol{U}}^{\top}\boldsymbol{K}_{\lambda}^{-1}{\boldsymbol{U}}=\penalty\

\displaystyle{\boldsymbol{U}}^{\top}\boldsymbol{K}_{*}^{-1}{\boldsymbol{U}}-{\boldsymbol{U}}^{\top}\boldsymbol{K}_{*}^{-1}{\boldsymbol{U}}({\boldsymbol{D}}^{-1}+{\boldsymbol{U}}^{\top}\boldsymbol{K}_{*}{\boldsymbol{U}})^{-1}{\boldsymbol{U}}^{\top}\boldsymbol{K}_{*}^{-1}{\boldsymbol{U}}

(340)

\displaystyle=\penalty\

\displaystyle({\mathbf{I}}-{\boldsymbol{U}}^{\top}\boldsymbol{K}_{*}^{-1}{\boldsymbol{U}}({\boldsymbol{D}}^{-1}+{\boldsymbol{U}}^{\top}\boldsymbol{K}_{*}{\boldsymbol{U}})^{-1}){\boldsymbol{U}}^{\top}\boldsymbol{K}_{*}^{-1}{\boldsymbol{U}}

(341)

\displaystyle=\penalty\

\displaystyle{\boldsymbol{D}}^{-1}({\boldsymbol{D}}^{-1}+{\boldsymbol{U}}^{\top}\boldsymbol{K}_{*}{\boldsymbol{U}})^{-1}{\boldsymbol{U}}^{\top}\boldsymbol{K}_{*}^{-1}{\boldsymbol{U}}

(342)

\displaystyle=\penalty\

\displaystyle{\boldsymbol{D}}^{-1}-{\boldsymbol{D}}^{-1}({\boldsymbol{D}}^{-1}+{\boldsymbol{U}}^{\top}\boldsymbol{K}_{*}{\boldsymbol{U}})^{-1}{\boldsymbol{D}}^{-1}

(343)

\displaystyle=\penalty\

\displaystyle{\boldsymbol{D}}^{-1}-({\boldsymbol{D}}+{\boldsymbol{D}}{\boldsymbol{U}}^{\top}\boldsymbol{K}_{*}{\boldsymbol{U}}{\boldsymbol{D}})^{-1}.

(344)

Lemma 47
.

Under the assumptions of Theorem
7
, we have

|\mathcal{R}_{1}|\lesssim d^{-0.4}
,
with probability at least
1-O(d^{-\frac{1}{48}})
,
where
\mathcal{R}_{1}
is defined in (
331
).

Proof.

Recall that
\boldsymbol{\mu}=d\cdot{\boldsymbol{f}}_{*}=[{\boldsymbol{x}}_{1}^{\top}{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}{\boldsymbol{x}}_{1},\ldots,{\boldsymbol{x}}_{n}^{\top}{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}{\boldsymbol{x}}_{n}]^{\top}
. Then
\mathbb{E}[\boldsymbol{\mu}]=\operatorname{Tr}({\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}^{2})\mathbf{1}
.
Define
\bar{\boldsymbol{\mu}}:=\boldsymbol{\mu}-\operatorname{Tr}({\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}^{2})\mathbf{1}
. Thus, (
235
) indicates that

\displaystyle\|\bar{\boldsymbol{\mu}}\|\lesssim d^{1.6},\quad\|\boldsymbol{\mu}\|\lesssim d^{2},

(345)

with probability at least
1-d^{-1}
.
Recall the definitions of
{\boldsymbol{U}}
and
{\boldsymbol{D}}
in (
337
) and (
338
). From the definition of
\mathcal{R}_{1}
, we can simplify it as

\displaystyle\mathcal{R}_{1}=\penalty\

\displaystyle\frac{1}{d^{2}}\big(\operatorname{Tr}({\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}^{2})-(a_{2}\boldsymbol{\mu}+a_{0}\mathbf{1})^{\top}\boldsymbol{K}_{\lambda}^{-1}\boldsymbol{\mu})^{2}

(346)

\displaystyle=\penalty\

\displaystyle\frac{1}{d^{2}}\left(\operatorname{Tr}({\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}^{2})-\begin{pmatrix}\frac{a_{0}}{\sqrt{a_{2}}}&\sqrt{a_{2}}\end{pmatrix}{\boldsymbol{U}}^{\top}\boldsymbol{K}_{\lambda}^{-1}{\boldsymbol{U}}\begin{pmatrix}0\\
\sqrt{a_{2}}\end{pmatrix}\right)^{2}.

(347)

Then, applying (
344
), we can get

\displaystyle\operatorname{Tr}({\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}^{2})-\begin{pmatrix}\frac{a_{0}}{\sqrt{a_{2}}}&\sqrt{a_{2}}\end{pmatrix}{\boldsymbol{U}}^{\top}\boldsymbol{K}_{\lambda}^{-1}{\boldsymbol{U}}\begin{pmatrix}0\\
\sqrt{a_{2}}\end{pmatrix}=\begin{pmatrix}\frac{a_{0}}{\sqrt{a_{2}}}&\sqrt{a_{2}}\end{pmatrix}({\boldsymbol{D}}+{\boldsymbol{D}}{\boldsymbol{U}}^{\top}\boldsymbol{K}_{*}{\boldsymbol{U}}{\boldsymbol{D}})^{-1}\begin{pmatrix}0\\
\sqrt{a_{2}}\end{pmatrix},

(348)

where we employ the identity:

\begin{pmatrix}\frac{a_{0}}{\sqrt{a_{2}}}&\sqrt{a_{2}}\end{pmatrix}{\boldsymbol{D}}^{-1}\begin{pmatrix}0\\
\sqrt{a_{2}}\end{pmatrix}=\operatorname{Tr}({\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}^{2})
.
Moreover, by calculation of the inverse of the
2\times 2
matrix, we know that

\displaystyle\begin{pmatrix}\frac{a_{0}}{\sqrt{a_{2}}}&\sqrt{a_{2}}\end{pmatrix}({\boldsymbol{D}}+{\boldsymbol{D}}{\boldsymbol{U}}^{\top}\boldsymbol{K}_{*}{\boldsymbol{U}}{\boldsymbol{D}})^{-1}\begin{pmatrix}0\\
\sqrt{a_{2}}\end{pmatrix}=

(349)

\displaystyle\frac{(a_{0}-a_{2}\operatorname{Tr}({\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}^{2}))(\mathbf{1}^{\top}\boldsymbol{K}_{*}^{-1}\bar{\boldsymbol{\mu}})+a_{2}\boldsymbol{\mu}^{\top}\boldsymbol{K}_{*}^{-1}\boldsymbol{\mu}-a_{2}\operatorname{Tr}({\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}^{2})\boldsymbol{\mu}^{\top}\boldsymbol{K}_{*}^{-1}\mathbf{1}}{-1-a_{0}\mathbf{1}^{\top}\boldsymbol{K}_{*}^{-1}\mathbf{1}+2a_{2}\mathbf{1}^{\top}\boldsymbol{K}_{*}^{-1}\bar{\boldsymbol{\mu}}-a_{2}\operatorname{Tr}({\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}^{2})\mathbf{1}^{\top}\boldsymbol{K}_{*}^{-1}\mathbf{1}+a_{2}^{2}(\boldsymbol{\mu}^{\top}\boldsymbol{K}_{*}^{-1}\boldsymbol{\mu}\cdot\mathbf{1}^{\top}\boldsymbol{K}_{*}^{-1}\mathbf{1}-(\mathbf{1}^{\top}\boldsymbol{K}_{*}^{-1}\boldsymbol{\mu})^{2})}.

(350)

Then, we control each term in the above fraction. For the numerator, by (
345
), we have

\displaystyle\big|(a_{0}-a_{2}\operatorname{Tr}({\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}^{2}))(\mathbf{1}^{\top}\boldsymbol{K}_{*}^{-1}\bar{\boldsymbol{\mu}})+a_{2}\boldsymbol{\mu}^{\top}\boldsymbol{K}_{*}^{-1}\boldsymbol{\mu}-a_{2}\operatorname{Tr}({\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}^{2})\boldsymbol{\mu}^{\top}\boldsymbol{K}_{*}^{-1}\mathbf{1}\big|\lesssim d^{2.6}

(351)

with probability at least
1-d^{-1}
. For the denominator, from (
339
), we can easily see that

\displaystyle O(d^{\frac{11}{6}})=nd^{-\frac{1}{6}}\lesssim a_{0}\mathbf{1}^{\top}\boldsymbol{K}_{*}^{-1}\mathbf{1}\lesssim d^{2},

(352)

with high probability. Meanwhile, by (
339
) and (
345
),

\displaystyle a_{2}|\mathbf{1}^{\top}\boldsymbol{K}_{*}^{-1}\bar{\boldsymbol{\mu}}|\lesssim d^{0.6},\quad a_{2}\operatorname{Tr}({\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}^{2})\mathbf{1}^{\top}\boldsymbol{K}_{*}^{-1}\mathbf{1}\lesssim d

(353)

with high probability. Lastly, (
339
) and (
345
) also indicate that

\displaystyle a_{2}^{2}(\boldsymbol{\mu}^{\top}\boldsymbol{K}_{*}^{-1}\boldsymbol{\mu}\cdot\mathbf{1}^{\top}\boldsymbol{K}_{*}^{-1}\mathbf{1}-(\mathbf{1}^{\top}\boldsymbol{K}_{*}^{-1}\boldsymbol{\mu})(\mathbf{1}^{\top}\boldsymbol{K}_{*}^{-1}\boldsymbol{\mu}))

(354)

\displaystyle=\penalty\

\displaystyle a_{2}^{2}(\boldsymbol{\mu}^{\top}\boldsymbol{K}_{*}^{-1}\boldsymbol{\mu}\cdot\mathbf{1}^{\top}\boldsymbol{K}_{*}^{-1}\mathbf{1}-(\mathbf{1}^{\top}\boldsymbol{K}_{*}^{-1}\bar{\boldsymbol{\mu}}+\operatorname{Tr}({\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}^{2})\cdot\mathbf{1}^{\top}\boldsymbol{K}_{*}^{-1}\mathbf{1})(\mathbf{1}^{\top}\boldsymbol{K}_{*}^{-1}\boldsymbol{\mu}))

(355)

\displaystyle=\penalty\

\displaystyle a_{2}^{2}(\bar{\boldsymbol{\mu}}^{\top}\boldsymbol{K}_{*}^{-1}\boldsymbol{\mu}\cdot\mathbf{1}^{\top}\boldsymbol{K}_{*}^{-1}\mathbf{1}-(\mathbf{1}^{\top}\boldsymbol{K}_{*}^{-1}\bar{\boldsymbol{\mu}})(\mathbf{1}^{\top}\boldsymbol{K}_{*}^{-1}\boldsymbol{\mu}))=O(d^{1.6})

(356)

with high probability. Combining (
352
), (
353
), and (
356
), we can get

\big|-1-a_{0}\mathbf{1}^{\top}\boldsymbol{K}_{*}^{-1}\mathbf{1}+2a_{2}\mathbf{1}^{\top}\boldsymbol{K}_{*}^{-1}\bar{\boldsymbol{\mu}}-a_{2}\operatorname{Tr}({\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}^{2})\mathbf{1}^{\top}\boldsymbol{K}_{*}^{-1}\mathbf{1}+a_{2}^{2}(\boldsymbol{\mu}^{\top}\boldsymbol{K}_{*}^{-1}\boldsymbol{\mu}\cdot\mathbf{1}^{\top}\boldsymbol{K}_{*}^{-1}\mathbf{1}-(\mathbf{1}^{\top}\boldsymbol{K}_{*}^{-1}\boldsymbol{\mu})^{2})\big|\geq d^{\frac{11}{6}}.

(357)

Therefore, with (
351
), we can conclude this lemma.
∎

Lemma 48
.

Under the assumptions of Theorem
7
, we have
|\mathcal{R}_{2}|\lesssim d^{-1/2}
,
with probability at least
1-O(d^{-1/2})
,
where
\mathcal{R}_{2}
is defined in (
332
).

Proof.

By the assumption of
{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}
, we know that
|\operatorname{Tr}[{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}^{4}]|\lesssim d
and
\boldsymbol{\mu}:=d{\boldsymbol{f}}_{*}
. Then for the second term in
\mathcal{R}_{2}
, we have

\displaystyle a_{2}^{2}{\boldsymbol{f}}_{*}^{\top}\boldsymbol{K}_{\lambda}^{-1}({\boldsymbol{X}}{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}{\boldsymbol{X}}^{\top})^{\odot 2}\boldsymbol{K}_{\lambda}^{-1}{\boldsymbol{f}}_{*}\lesssim\penalty\

\displaystyle\frac{1}{d^{4}}\boldsymbol{\mu}^{\top}\boldsymbol{K}_{\lambda}^{-1}a_{2}({\boldsymbol{X}}{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}{\boldsymbol{X}}^{\top})^{\odot 2}\boldsymbol{K}_{\lambda}^{-1}\boldsymbol{\mu}\lesssim\penalty\ \frac{1}{d^{4}}\boldsymbol{\mu}^{\top}\boldsymbol{K}_{\lambda}^{-1}\boldsymbol{\mu}\lesssim\frac{1}{d}

(358)

with probability at least
1-O(d^{-\frac{1}{2}})
, where we employ Lemmas
26
and
38
.
Lastly, in the third term of
\mathcal{R}_{2}
, by the definition of
{\boldsymbol{v}}_{0}^{(2)}
in (
318
), with a slight modification of Lemma
38
, we can derive
\frac{4a_{2}}{d}|{\boldsymbol{v}}_{0}^{(2)}\boldsymbol{K}_{\lambda}^{-1}{\boldsymbol{f}}_{*}|\lesssim\frac{1}{d^{4}}|{\boldsymbol{v}}_{0}^{(2)}\boldsymbol{K}_{\lambda}^{-1}\boldsymbol{\mu}|\lesssim\frac{1}{d}

with probability at least
1-O(d^{-\frac{1}{2}})
.
∎

Lemma 49
.

Under the assumptions of Theorem
7
, we have

|\mathcal{R}_{\textnormal{mix}}|\lesssim d^{-0.3}
,
with probability at least
1-O(d^{-\frac{1}{48}})
,
where

\mathcal{R}_{\textnormal{mix}}
is defined by (
335
).

Proof.

We control the terms in (
335
), respectively. Firstly, recall
\widetilde{\boldsymbol{b}}:=a_{2}\boldsymbol{\mu}+a_{0}\mathbf{1}
from (
336
) and
{\boldsymbol{b}}_{0}
from Lemma
205
. Then, for any
i\in[n]
, the
i
-th entry

\displaystyle({\boldsymbol{b}}_{0}-\widetilde{\boldsymbol{b}})_{i}=\frac{f^{(4)}(0)}{8d^{4}}(t_{i}-\operatorname{Tr}({\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}^{2}))^{2}+\frac{15t_{i}^{3}f^{(6)}(0)}{6!d^{6}}.

(359)

Therefore, by (
210
), we know that

\|{\boldsymbol{b}}_{0}-\widetilde{\boldsymbol{b}}\|\lesssim d^{-1.9}
, with probability at least
1-O(d^{-1})
. Hence, by (
345
) and (
109
), we have

|{\boldsymbol{f}}_{*}^{\top}\boldsymbol{K}_{\lambda}^{-1}({\boldsymbol{b}}_{0}-\widetilde{\boldsymbol{b}})|\lesssim\frac{1}{d}\left\|\boldsymbol{\mu}\right\|\cdot\left\|{\boldsymbol{b}}_{0}-\widetilde{\boldsymbol{b}}\right\|\lesssim d^{-0.9}.

Moreover, Lemma
38
verifies that with probability at least
1-O(d^{-1/2})
,

|{\boldsymbol{f}}_{*}^{\top}\boldsymbol{K}_{\lambda}^{-1}\widetilde{\boldsymbol{b}}|\lesssim\frac{1}{d^{3}}\boldsymbol{\mu}^{\top}\boldsymbol{K}_{\lambda}^{-1}\boldsymbol{\mu}+\frac{1}{d}|\mathbf{1}^{\top}\boldsymbol{K}_{\lambda}^{-1}\boldsymbol{\mu}|\lesssim d^{0.6}.

Thus, combining all the above, we have with probability at least
1-O(d^{-1/2})
,

\displaystyle\big|{\boldsymbol{f}}_{*}^{\top}\boldsymbol{K}_{\lambda}^{-1}({\boldsymbol{b}}_{0}{\boldsymbol{b}}_{0}^{\top}-\widetilde{\boldsymbol{b}}\widetilde{\boldsymbol{b}}_{0}^{\top})\boldsymbol{K}_{\lambda}^{-1}{\boldsymbol{f}}_{*}\big|\leq

\displaystyle|{\boldsymbol{f}}_{*}^{\top}\boldsymbol{K}_{\lambda}^{-1}({\boldsymbol{b}}_{0}-\widetilde{\boldsymbol{b}})|^{2}+|{\boldsymbol{f}}_{*}^{\top}\boldsymbol{K}_{\lambda}^{-1}({\boldsymbol{b}}_{0}-\widetilde{\boldsymbol{b}})||{\boldsymbol{f}}_{*}^{\top}\boldsymbol{K}_{\lambda}^{-1}\widetilde{\boldsymbol{b}}|\lesssim d^{-0.3}.

(360)

Similarly, we can verify

\big|\frac{\operatorname{Tr}({\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}^{2})}{d}({\boldsymbol{b}}_{0}-\widetilde{\boldsymbol{b}})^{\top}\boldsymbol{K}_{\lambda}^{-1}{\boldsymbol{f}}_{*}\big|\lesssim d^{-0.9}
.
Next, by (
109
), Lemmas
32
,
34
and
38
, we have with probability at least
1-O(d^{-\frac{1}{48}})
,

\displaystyle{\boldsymbol{f}}_{*}^{\top}\boldsymbol{K}_{\lambda}^{-1}\text{diag}({\boldsymbol{b}}_{1}-\widetilde{\boldsymbol{b}}_{1}){\boldsymbol{X}}{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}{\boldsymbol{X}}^{\top}\text{diag}({\boldsymbol{b}}_{1}-\widetilde{\boldsymbol{b}}_{1})\boldsymbol{K}_{\lambda}^{-1}{\boldsymbol{f}}_{*}

(361)

\displaystyle\leq\penalty\

\displaystyle\frac{1}{d^{2}}\boldsymbol{\mu}^{\top}\boldsymbol{K}_{\lambda}^{-1}\boldsymbol{\mu}\cdot\max_{i\in[n]}|b_{1,i}-\widetilde{b}_{1,i}|^{2}\cdot\|{\boldsymbol{X}}{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}{\boldsymbol{X}}^{\top}\|\lesssim d^{-2}.

(362)

Moreover, Lemma
39
shows that
\operatorname{Tr}\boldsymbol{K}_{\lambda}^{-1}{\boldsymbol{b}}_{0}{\boldsymbol{b}}_{0}^{\top}\boldsymbol{K}_{\lambda}^{-1}={\boldsymbol{b}}_{0}^{\top}\boldsymbol{K}_{\lambda}^{-2}{\boldsymbol{b}}_{0}\lesssim d^{-0.8}
with probability
1-O(d^{-\frac{1}{48}})
. Lastly, by (
109
), Lemmas
32
,
34
and
38
, with probability
1-O(d^{-\frac{1}{48}})
,

\displaystyle\operatorname{Tr}\boldsymbol{K}_{\lambda}^{-1}\text{diag}({\boldsymbol{b}}_{1}-\widetilde{\boldsymbol{b}}_{1}){\boldsymbol{X}}{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}{\boldsymbol{X}}^{\top}\text{diag}({\boldsymbol{b}}_{1}-\widetilde{\boldsymbol{b}}_{1})\boldsymbol{K}_{\lambda}^{-1}

(363)

\displaystyle\leq\penalty\

\displaystyle\sqrt{d}\|\boldsymbol{K}_{\lambda}^{-1}\|^{2}\cdot\|{\boldsymbol{X}}{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}{\boldsymbol{X}}^{\top}\|\cdot\max_{i\in[n]}|b_{1,i}-\widetilde{b}_{1,i}|^{2}\lesssim d^{-3}.

(364)

∎

Proof of Theorem
7

Combining Lemmas
46
,
47
,
48
, and
49
, we can obtain that

|\mathcal{R}(\lambda)-\sigma_{\varepsilon}^{2}{\mathcal{V}}|\lesssim d^{-1/4}
,
with probability at least
1-O(d^{-1/48})
for any
\lambda\geq 0
.
Here we utilized the decomposition of
\widetilde{}\mathcal{R}(\lambda)
in (
330
). Hence, it suffices to analyze the limit of the variance term
{\mathcal{V}}
defined in Lemma
42
. Because of Lemma
43
and the approximation of
{\mathcal{V}}_{0}
in (
310
), we can copy the analysis of
{\mathcal{V}}_{0}
in the proof of Theorem
4
to conclude that

|\mathcal{R}(\lambda)-\sigma_{\varepsilon}^{2}{\mathcal{V}}(\lambda_{*})|\to 0
,
in probability, as
d\to\infty
and
d^{2}/(2n)\to\alpha
, for any
\lambda\geq 0
, where
{\mathcal{V}}(\lambda_{*})
is defined in (
147
). This completes the proof of
Theorem
7
.

E.4
Proof of Corollary
9

Based on the proof of Theorem
3
and Theorem
2
, we have

\displaystyle\frac{1}{n}{\boldsymbol{y}}^{\top}(\boldsymbol{K}+\lambda{\mathbf{I}})^{-2}{\boldsymbol{y}}

\displaystyle\to\lambda^{2}\int\frac{\frac{1}{\alpha}x+\sigma_{\boldsymbol{\epsilon}}^{2}}{\left(\frac{f^{\prime\prime}(0)}{4\alpha}x+a_{*}+\lambda\right)^{2}}\penalty\ d\mu_{\alpha,{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}^{(2)}}(x),

(365)

\displaystyle\frac{1}{n}\operatorname{Tr}((\boldsymbol{K}+\lambda{\mathbf{I}})^{-1})

\displaystyle\to\left(\frac{4\alpha}{f^{\prime\prime}(0)}\right)\cdot\int\frac{1}{(x+\frac{4\alpha}{f^{\prime\prime}(0)}(a_{*}+\lambda))}d\mu_{{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}^{(2)}}(x),

(366)

in probability. For simplicity, we denote
A=\frac{4\alpha}{f^{\prime\prime}(0)}
and
z=-A(a_{*}+\lambda)
. Let the Stieltjes transform of
\mu_{{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}^{(2)}}
be
m(z)
. Then, we have

\displaystyle\mathrm{GCV_{\lambda}(\boldsymbol{K},{\boldsymbol{y}})}

\displaystyle\to\frac{A}{\alpha}\left(-z\frac{m^{\prime}(-z)}{m(-z)}+1+\frac{1}{2z}-\frac{1}{2zm(-z)}\right)+\sigma_{\boldsymbol{\epsilon}}^{2}A\frac{m^{\prime}(-z)}{m(-z)}.

(367)

in probability as
n\to\infty
. Recall the companion Stieltjes transform
\widetilde{m}(z)
for
m(z)
defined in Definition
11
and the relation between
m(z)
and
\widetilde{m}(z)
:
\widetilde{m}(z):=\alpha m(z)+(1-\alpha)(-1/z).

Then we can rewrite (
367
) in terms of
\widetilde{m}(z)
.
Then, we can apply (4) and Lemma 2.2 by
Dobriban and Wager (2018)
, and the proof of Theorem
4
to conclude the proof.
